### Page 21

Global Market Outlook 21

Summary

Our stock-bond model (3-6 months) reduced its Overweight allocation to global equities to +1 from +3 (maximum +5) 
in September. The downgrade was driven primarily by a weaker valuation signal as valuation score fell to -2 for both Developed 
Market and Asian equities. Despite some moderation, fundamentals remain strongly supportive of equities. Positive factors 
include resilient net earnings upgrades, a positive economic surprise index, and PMI new orders remaining above 50, signalling 
continued expansion in industrial activity. Market technicals weakened but continued to support equities overall. Our market 
breadth indicator shows that 72% of equity markets remain above their 200 -day moving averages. Stock net advances are 
positive and not at levels that would typically signal an imminent market reversal. The stock-bond model continues to outperform 
strongly, generating 4.2% alpha YTD relative to the 60/40 equity-bond benchmark. 
Our 1–3 month equity market regime models continue to signal low bear market risk, with probabilities of just 0.7% for 
S&P 500 and 5.0% for MSCI AC World  indices. The low readings continued to be driven by subdued option -market implied 
volatility. At 14 – 15%, the VIX and market cap -weighted one-month implied volatility measures for Europe and US remain 
comfortably below the 20% threshold at which bear market risk tends to rise sharply. While momentum indicators have softened, 
they point to a typical market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful market stress.  
Our positioning indicators are not signalling any reversal risk. Previous long positioning in DXY has fully unwound and our 
market diversity indicator for MSCI Singapore has also normalized after the index corrected from its September peak.

15 Quant perspective: Reducing Equity 
Overweight as Valuation Weakened

Francis Lim Senior Quantitative Strategist Maggie, Au Yeung Quantitative Analyst

### Page 22

Global Market Outlook 22 
Fig. 36 Long- and short-term quantitative models remain bullish on risk assets 
Long-term models below have a typical time horizon of 3-6 months, while short-term models have a 1-3-month horizon

Long-term Stock or bond US recession model 
Current view Reduce equity Overweight 20% probability within the next 6 months 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Fundamental: +2. 3-month change in PMI new 
orders indicate a slower pace in the expansion of 
industrial activity. All other factors such as net 
earnings upgrades, economic surprise index and 
risk sentiment remain positive.  
• Valuation: -2. Both DM and Asian equities are 
expensive as their valuation scores exceeded 1 
standard deviation of historical average.  
• Technical: +1. Net-advances in global stocks are 
positive but not extreme. Market breadth declined 
as equity markets above their 200-day moving 
averages fell from 85% to 72%, but it remains 
above the model’s 60% threshold. 
Estimated probability hovers around 20 – 21% 
• Conference Board consumer confidence 
remains weak at -8.6% y/y. 
• PMI new orders-to-inventory ratio is at 1.1x, 
below its historical average of 1.2x.  
• Conference Board leading indicators 
contracted by -1.1% y/y.  
• Fed funds rate-to-natural rate ratio is still high 
at 1.6x, reflecting a restrictive monetary policy 
compared to historical average of 1.3x. 
Key model 
factors 
• Economic activity, macro risk and surprise indices, 
corporate earnings, forward P/E ratio and technical 
factors. 
• Conference Board leading indicator, Consumer 
Confidence Index, ISM New Orders and Fed 
funds rate. 
How does it 
work? 
• A monthly scorecard of -5 to 5 based on 
fundamentals, valuations and market breadth 
factors to indicate the relative preference for bonds 
and equities. A positive score favours equities and 
vice versa. 
• A probability model based on regression. Long-
term statistical tests are conducted to identify 
the four key economic factors above.

Short-term Technical analysis Investor positioning 
Current views Bullish S&P 500 & MSCI AC World indices None flagged 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Bullish S&P500 and MSCI AC World. The 
probabilities of bear market sit at just 0.7% and 
5.0%, respectively, driven by subdued options-
market implied volatility. Momentum across both 
indices have weakened but they point to a typical 
market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful  
market stress.  
• Previous long positioning in the USD index 
(DXY) has fully unwound after DXY fell 
steeply form its peak in late July. The renewed 
strength in DXY since the Fed’s 16 Sept rate 
hike has started from neutral positioning.  
• Market diversity indicator for MSCI 
Singapore has normalised after the index 
corrected from its September peak. 
Key model 
factors 
• Market factors: Momentum, volatility, interest rate 
differentials, relative returns, inflation swap rates, 
economic surprises, etc. 
• Price action: Overbought conditions occur 
when prices rise sharply; oversold conditions 
happen when prices fall rapidly in a short time.  
How does it 
work? 
• Scanning through 7,000+ factors, the framework 
uses Machine Learning to forecast market regimes 
or future trends based on identified market drivers. 
• A market indicator based on fractal analysis 
that provides a timely indication of investor 
positioning based on price actions.

Source: Standard Chartered

### Page 27

Global Market Outlook 27

Time is your most precious commodity – be sure to spend it wisely 
Time is undoubtedly valuable. The days may seem long, but the years are short. So, make the choice 
to spend your time wisely. Whether you’re setting out on your investment journey, navigating the 
intricacies of mid -life wealth planning or fortifying assets  for your golden years, invest time today to 
ensure your wealth strategy is aligned to what’s right for you – Today, Tomorrow and Forever. 
Setting aside the time now to review your plan will pay dividends in the future. Markets have moved. 
Your portfolio’s current asset allocation may no longer be optimally positioned to maximise the 
opportunities ahead. Ask yourself the following: Am I holding too much cash? Am I sufficiently allocating 
to growth assets for the long term? Is my portfolio diversified? Am I capturing the best opportunities? 
And most importantly, is my wealth working hard for me so that I don’t have to? 
Use our SC Wealth Select framework and advisory specialists to help guide you through this process.

Purpose 
Today,  
Tomorrow,  
Forever 
Our approach to helping you grow and manage 
your wealth starts with you. We use a goals -
aware approach to understand your vision of 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever for yourself, your 
family and beyond, and then design portfolios to 
meet your various needs.  
Using our ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ 
approach, we ensure your wealth needs for the 
near term (Today) are met, while ensuring your 
wealth needs for the decades ahead (Tomorrow 
and Forever) are also planned for.  
Your vision of ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ is 
unique to you. Our specialists partner with you to 
build well -diversified, long -term Foundation 
portfolios, aligned to your Today, Tomorrow and 
Forever needs. Opportunistic ideas are added to 
capture short -term opportunities, a nd sufficient 
protection is included to address the objectives of 
you and your family. 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever 
Approach 
Planning for Today 
Requires ensuring liquidity and income 
flows take centre stage 
Securing Tomorrow 
Entails a well-diversified investment and 
protection portfolio with a focus on growth, 
ensuring inflation is accounted for and risks 
are mitigated 
Building for Forever 
Involves greater focus on long-term returns 
given the time horizon of your portfolio can 
be measured in decades, and might also 
include business interests, real estate, 
collectibles or charitable funds

SC Wealth Select  
 Managing your wealth through the decades – Today, Tomorrow and Forever

### Page 28

Global Market Outlook 28

Principles 
that stand the 
test of time 
Adhering to time-tested principles, to ensure your 
investment decisions remain robust and 
consistently applied, is paramount to your 
success Today, Tomorrow and Forever. We use 
five Wealth Principles to guide and guardrail your 
wealth decisions.

Discipline – ensure consistency and 
prudence over your emotions 
• Reacting to emotions, such as optimism and 
fear, can lead to poor investment decisions at 
the worst times 
• Have a plan and stick to it – this helps you to 
stay focused on the bigger picture

Diversification – simply put, don’t put 
all your eggs in one basket 
• Reduce risk by holding a variety of financial 
assets. Multi -asset diversification in your 
Foundation portfolio is important 
• As a guide, make sure your portfolio contains 
a variety of asset classes and investments 
that have low correlation with one another

Time in the Market – a more robust 
strategy than timing the market

• Predicting market selloffs is challenging, and 
timing your exit and re-entry is difficult 
• Missing out on the best performing days of a 
market can have a significantly detrimental 
impact on your portfolio 
• ‘Time in the market’ and buying the market 
with a longer -term view provide more 
consistent returns that can ride out bumps 
along the way

Risk and Return – make sure the risk 
is worth the return 
• To achieve higher investment returns, you will 
likely have to accept a greater level of risk in 
your portfolio 
• Therefore, it’s important to understand the 
risks and manage these on an ongoing basis

Protection – don’t let the unexpected 
catch you unprepared 
• Even though you may feel healthy, or 
financially stable now, protection offers the 
ability to overcome times of financial 
uncertainty and mitigate the long-term impact 
of unforeseen events on your wealth 
• A good protection plan not only safeguards 
your wealth today, but also considers the 
value of your future earnings over your 
lifetime, in today’s terms

holistic

needs change. Hence, we encourage you to 
undertake regular portfolio reviews to ensure your 
portfolio remains aligned to your Today, 
Tomorrow and Forever objectives. This proactive 
approach includes strategic reb alancing based 
on insights from our Chief Investment Office.

Sector Report | Technology｜Oct 15, 2026 
Oct Edition: Choppy Markets, Intact AI

Overseas  
Technology

Jeff Pu, CFA 
SFC CE No. BNO719 
jeffpu@gfgroup.com.hk 
Henry Huang 
SFC CE No. BYA749 
henryhuang@gfgroup.com.hk

Market to remain choppy: Tech equities were volatile in the past month, with rotation 
toward hyperscaler and internet names. That said , s emi and memory (Korea) 
weakness reflected lower risk appetite due to rising bond yields, AI monetization/ARR 
visibility and L-T AI debates (i.e., Broadcom CEO warned that capital may now be a 
bigger constraint), even as company-specific catalysts continued to be strong. On the 
other hand, the positive was the acceleration of AI model scaling (i.e., Meta’s Muse 
Wonder, Gemini 4, etc). That said, w e view the pullback primarily as a reset in 
expectations rather than the evidence of AI deterioration. Despite light positioning, we 
expect the markets will remain choppy in the near term but would  accumulate low 
valuation & sustainable names  (i.e., HBM, non-TSMC foundries, CPUs, CPOs, etc) . 
In our preferred list, we added CLS, while removing MTK and Hon Hai.  
U.S. roadshow takeaways (week of Oct 5): Investor tone skewed neutral to slightly 
cautious, driven by conservative top ‑down macro views, U.S. election, and ongoing 
debates around the L-T AI demand. Positioning in AI infrastructure has come off peak 
levels. Even  so, many investors would accumulate on pullbacks, with interest 
concentrated in “Made in U.S.” names (INTC, LITE, COHR), U.S. memory suppliers, 
TSMC, and selective bottleneck themes — including ASE, ABF substrates, 
MRVL/SMTC, and Japanese MLCCs. 
OCP & earnings season in focus: The week is anchored by AI/semis — SEMICON 
West, OCP, and earnings from ASML, TSMC, etc. OCP reinforced the industry’s pivot 
toward rack‑scale infrastructure, with inference and agentic workloads driving demand 
across compute, memory, and networking. Scale‑up optics, NPO/CPO, and 800VDC 
power were key themes, while AMD’s CPU outlook and Helios/Helion underscored a 
secular CPU uptrend. Nvidia ’s use of PTFE for NVLink boards further supports our 
positive stance on PTFE (see PCB Spec in 2 H27, Oct 5).  On earnings, ASML 
delivered largely in -line results, GM and OP above consensus with revenue slightly 
miss, and raised full year guidance again and is exploring ways to increase capacity 
by >30% 2028 to fulfill strong customer demand, also extending the order visibility 
beyond 2028. For TSMC, we anticipate an inline quarter. 
September monthly sales/data: TSMC sales -0.6% MoM, slightly ahead. Rubin to 
accelerate and T3 ramp. PC forecast trimmed. Substrate/CCL upcycle with 
pricing/margin expansion. Aspeed revenue +101% YoY. Details on page 5. 
GFHK Tech Most Read:  1) Jeff Pu on ASIC Updates on TPU v9 ; 2) Michelle Jing 
on PCB Spec in 2H27; 3) Alicia Xia on CPO Acceleration; 4) Jeff Pu on NAND Re-
acceleration; 5) Jeff Pu on TSMC 3Q26 Preview.

### Page 3

Sector Report | Technology

GFHK Tech Important Stock Calls  
In the past one month, our key reports and stock calls are: 
Jeff Pu on ASIC Updates on TPU v9, reported date on Oct 13: We expect near-term MTK headwinds 
from TPU v9 delays but remain constructive on MTK/MRVL longer term. Humufish’s redesign could 
push tape-out to Jan -27, MP tape -out to 2Q27 and shipments to 1Q28, while EMIB -T yields could 
recover from 45–50%. Rising TPU allocations to Anthropic could constrain Gemini compute, though 
higher internal allocation in 2H26 may support future models. SemiAnalysis expects Anthropic/OpenAI 
to each add 5 –10GW in 2027 vs. a few GW for Google. For TPU v10 (4Q28 /1Q29), MTK/GUC’s 
Icefish remains the only confirmed mainstream SKU, while Broadcom-led Merope faces AMD/Marvell 
competition, with both potentially testing EMIB-T. 
Michelle Jing on PCB Spec in 2H27, reported date on Oct 5: Some market watchers expect Nvidia 
to shift from PTFE to hydrocarbon resin (HC) for the VR300 NVL576 switch tray. HC—one of the glass 
free alternatives within PTFE hybrid architectures—has only recently passed SI testing and has yet to 
enter PCB MP, leaving it roughly three months behind PTFE in qualification. On the contrary, we 
believe that PTFE offers superior performance for high frequency, high speed designs. With VR300 
NVL576 shipments expected in 2H27 and the final configuration likely set by end 26 —and with a 
leading Chinese PCB ma ker already achieving meaningful PTFE hybrid yields —we maintain that 
PTFE hybrid remains the most probable solution for next year’s switch board. 
Alicia Xia on CPO Acceleration , reported date on  Sept 29 : Nvidia’s CPO scale -out switch is 
progressing faster than expected, with FAU yields improving. We forecast CPO switch shipments at 
15k/16k/30k in 4Q26/1Q27/2Q27 and 100k in 2027. For Nvidia/AWS scale -up, CPO and NPO 
continue in parallel, with full NPO adoption expected at NVL576 launch. Feynman is also devel oping 
both variants, with CPO potentially using QM6 + six 6.4T OEs. We shift AWS Trainium 4 launch to 
1Q28 and forecast global scale-up OE demand at 6.5m/42m in 2027E/28E. 
Jeff Pu on NAND Re -acceleration, reported date on Sept 20 : According to TrendForce, NAND 
demand is stronger than expected as U.S. CSPs scale AI infrastructure, with eSSD demand nearly 
2x prior forecasts. We raise NAND contract price growth to +21%/+17%/+15% QoQ in 
3Q26/4Q26/1Q27, while hyperscaler inventories decline. Supply remains disci plined at 15%/21%/20% 
YoY bit growth in 2025 –27. Demand is supported by agentic AI, vector databases and KV -cache 
offloading, where eSSD offers lower cost/bit than DRAM and better power efficiency than HDD. 
Jeff Pu on TSMC 3Q26 Preview , reported date on  Oct 7 : TSMC shares hit record highs on 
expectations ahead of the print and potential Terafab collaboration. We expect largely inline 3Q26 
results, capex and full-year guidance, with softer 4Q26 margin (67.2%). Management should reaffirm 
2026 capex at the ceiling and mid-40s YoY growth, while we forecast 37% growth in 2027 on sustained 
AI/CPU/networking demand and price hikes. Key focuses include margins, long -term capex/U.S. 
expansion, large-reticle packaging and A14. We raise 2026E/27E EPS by 2%/8% on hig her N2/N3 
capacity and operating leverage, and roll to 20x 2027E P/E, lifting TP to NT$2,960.

September Monthly Data  
TSMC September sales of NT$511.9bn, -0.6% MoM and +54.6% YoY, with total 3Q revenue reaching 
NT$1.49T, +17.6 QoQ, slightly ahead of expectations of +14%. The solid performance was well 
supported by ASIC, A20 Pro, and Rubin. For 3Q26, we expect gross margin to be 67.8%, higher than 
guidance of 65-67% and consensus of 66.4% . For 4Q26, we expect another 12% QoQ growth with 
GM at 67.2%, both ahead of street’s estimates of +10% QoQ and 66% margin. Ahead of the earnings 
call, key focus include gross margin trajectory, long -term capex discipline, and the execution of its 
U.S. fab expansion. 
Rubin to accelerate:  Hon Hai revenue last month was NT$1.16T, +26 % MoM, +38% YoY, mainly 
driven by iPhone build.  Quanta and Wistron delivered -23% and -17% MoM respectively, due to AI 
racks’ shipping timing differences. Of note, Pegatron surprised the market with +143.2% MoM revenue 
surge fueled by initial GB300 rack shipments to neocloud customers including Lambda and Together 
AI. This reaffirms our thesis of robust NVL72 order momentum across Neoclouds. Looking ahead, 
VR200 is ramping now, with volume deployment accelerating into 2027. We maintain our forecast of 
75k and 92k NVL72 (GB+VR) rack shipments for 2026 and 2027, respectively. Concurrently, Wiwynn 
delivered another solid month with revenue expanding 20.0% MoM, underpinne d by resilient GP 
server demand alongside the ramp of Trainium 3. 
ASIC ramping up: On design service vendors fronts, driven  by T3 ramp, Alchip’s Sep revenue 
reached a record NT$9.9 8bn. Backed by secured incremental 3nm wafer allocation and improving 
yields, management raised its 2027 revenue guidance, now targeting growth above the previous 50% 
YoY baseline. That said, we push out T4 shipment timeline into 1Q28. GUC delivered a strong beat 
with monthly revenue reaching a record NT$7.145bn (+22.4% MoM, +97.8 % YoY), driven by robust 
Axion 2 CPU demand. Beyond its engagement with top 4 CSPs, multi -year order visibility into 2027 
and beyond is further bolstered by high-margin (likely TK1) automotive ASIC designs. 
PC forecast trimmed: NB shipments from top 5 ODMs were 9.7m in Sep, with 3Q26 shipments down 
17% QoQ and 26% YoY. Global PC OEM shipments contracted 20.1% YoY in 3Q26, worse than 
expected, post robust 1H26 pull -ins with high pricing points and weak macro. For 2027, we project 
the industry will decline a further 5% YoY. On the competitive front, tier -1 vendors such as Lenovo, 
Dell, and HP are not immune to this downturn, while entry-level laptops (low cost models from China) 
continue to see stronger relative demand.  
Substrate/CCL Upcycle Intact: Unimicron continued to deliver another all-time high sales with +5% 
MoM and 65% YoY. Supported by ABF upward pricing of ~15% in 2H26 (likely more next year) and 
higher mix of AI products, we expect a meaningful GM uptick for this quarter with momentum to sustain 
into 2027 . In the meantime, ZengDing’s revenue rose 28% MoM and 36% YoY mainly driven by  
iPhone, 1.6T mSAP, and Rubin’s HDI. On the CCL front, bolstered by ongoing price hikes and strong 
demand from GP servers, ITEC revenue grew 15.5% MoM and 111% YoY, and NanYa Plastics 
recorded +12.5% MoM and +55% YoY. Meanwhile, EMC and TUC both posted another single digit 
growth MoM on sales.

### Page 11

Sector Report | Technology

Downstream tech – Smartphone & Apple  
We maintain Global smartphone forecast of -14% in 2026  
According to IDC, the preliminary 2Q26 smartphone data points to a still ‑soft market with uneven 
regional dynamics, and their commentary continues to emphasize structural rather than cyclical 
headwinds. Smartphone shipments remain under pressure from weak replacement cycles, cautious 
consumer spending, and limited breakthrough features. IDC typically highlights that “growth is 
concentrated in the premium tier,” driven by ecosystem lock ‑in, while the mass market remains 
challenged by price sensitivity. They also tend to note that China and parts of emerging markets show 
only modest stabilization, not a full recovery, and that vendors are prioritizing margin discipline over 
volume.  
Overall, by factoring in higher iPhone 17/17 Pro volume, we fine tune our global smartphone shipment 
forecast to -14% YoY from -15% YoY. 
For 2027, we expect global smartphone shipments to be flattish YoY. That said, 2026 -2027 will be 
one of the most challenging periods for the industry, driven primarily by sharply higher memory prices, 
which have already pushed OEMs to reduce shipments and raise device prices.

iPhone 18 Pro/18 PM demand lukewarm  
iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max wait-time lukewarm: iPhone 18 Pro and iPhone 18 Pro Max pre-orders began 
on Sept 12 in the U.S. and many other countries. Generally speaking, the wait-time appears lukewarm, 
with: 1) 3–4 weeks for 256GB/512GB Pro Max across all colors, as well as Burgundy 1TB in the U.S.；
2) 2–3 weeks for Glacier 256GB/512GB Pro Max in China; 3) 1–2 weeks for Black/Glacier 1TB Pro 
Max in the U.S. ；4) immediate availability for Silver 1TB and mos t 2TB Pro Max models in the U.S, 
as well as most iPhone 18 Pro configurations except for Burgundy 256GB in the U.S. We attribute the 
lukewarm wait-time to limited spec upgrade, higher pricing (especially for large storage models), etc. 
We also expect the Pro Max to be impacted by iPhone Duo and the criticism of heavy weight.  
iPhone Duo feedback generally positive 
The iPhone Duo, priced at $1,999 in the U.S., opened pre‑orders on Oct 16 and officially launched on 
Oct 23. Early reviews were positive, highlig hting its polished folding experience, high ‑quality inner 
display, and improved multitasking in iOS 27, but also point to trade ‑offs including a heavier and 
thicker form factor, a visible crease, and a dual ‑camera. Due to hinge constraints, we are reducing  
the iPhone Duo EMS build plan from 7m to 6m units in 2026. We also lower the iPhone 18 Pro/Pro 
Max build plan to 72m units given the constraints associated with the adjustable‑aperture camera.

Downstream tech – PC 
Global PC units down to -12% in 2026, followed by another decline in 2027 
On Oct 8, IDC reported 3Q26 global PC shipments fell 20.1% YoY, missing street expectations of a 
10–15% decline. The sharper contraction was primarily driven by front -loaded demand in 1H26, 
elevated ASPs, and broader macro weakness. This aligns with our prior view that ODM build 
momentum moderated starting in end -2Q26, amid the rising concerns over channel inventory. 
Meanwhile, the top 5 ODMs also saw 3Q26 shipments decline 26% YoY. Looking ahead, we model 
4Q26 shipments to continue 20% YoY drop (flattish QoQ), underperforming normal seasonality. 
On the component front, we expect memory de -spec to normalize. End -users still require sufficient 
DRAM capacity (16GB+) to handle on -device AI workloads, and PC DDR 5 supply constraints have 
eased compared to Server’s. On pricing, new models will still have higher pricing points to pass 
through 2H component cost increases across memory, CPU, etc. That said, we anticipate price hikes 
will remain measured vs 1H26 given sluggish end-market demand. 
Overall, reflecting weaker than expected 3Q26 results post pull -ins and a muted 4Q outlook, we trim 
our 2026 PC shipment forecast to -12% YoY. For 2027, we project shipments to decline further by 5% 
YoY, as higher ASP and persistent macro headwinds continue to weigh on demand.

Upstream tech – AI Semi & Supply Chain  
U.S. CSP capex to be +85%/+53%/+20% in 2026/2027/2028 
Given capex expands further into 2027, CSPs free cash flow are undergoing significant pressure and 
turning negative except Microsoft, such that shifting to debt financing for their massive capex. In 2025, 
Top 5 CSPs’ total debt issuance reached $108bn, or 24% of total capex . Per our Addressing 2028 
Capex and AI Platform Value Shift report on Aug 6, we forecast Top 5 CSPs’ debt issuance to reach 
$257bn/$419bn in 2026/2027, or 31%/35% of total capex.  Assuming debt issuance to grow in 
2028/2029 at a mild pace, we believe U.S. CSPs' balance sheet (except Oracle) to remain very healthy, 
due to net leverage (EBITDA basis) of only 0.5 and the net debt-to-equity ratio of only 18% by 2029.  
Despite healthy balance sheet for most CSPs, we expect most to safeguard balance sheets beyond 
2027 amid widening spreads and FCF profiles, with the focus shifting to returns. Together with a swing 
factor of competition from Chinese LLMs, we expect U.S. CSPs to decelerate capex expansion. We 
forecast Top 5 CSPs capex of $1,512bn/1,739bn in 2028/2029, or +20%/15% YoY, down from +53% 
in 2027.

Server CPU TAM to grow at 49% CAGR by 2030 
As the orchestration core of AI infrastructure buildouts, the Server CPU has gained significant traction 
over the past few months. This is driven by the Agentic AI/inference trend, fueled by the breakthrough 
of OpenClaw and Anthropic’s ARR surging to $44bn  in April. For instance, in latest earnings calls, 
AMD projected a >35% CAGR for Server CPU TAM by 2030, and Intel said the shift toward agentic 
AI is driving a structural demand for CPU, where the GPU-to-CPU ratio is tightening from 8:1 in training 
to 4:1 in inference. Based on our estimates, assuming inference accounts for 90% of AI workloads 
and GPU-to-CPU ratio for AI servers to reach 1:1.2 in 2030, we now expect Server CPU TAM to grow 
96%/71% in 2026/2027, reaching $215bn by 2030 from $30bn in 2025, with a 5-yr CAGR of 49%.

Upstream tech – Optical 
Strong demand for pluggable through 2028. 
We expect that the total demand for 800G/1.6T will reach 90mn/80mn in 2027, increased by 
80%/167%+, mainly driven by: 1) Nvidia, Google and AWS and other ASIC’s Accelerators’ demand 
are ramping up; 2) GPU/ASIC scale -out bandwidth continuously  increase drives increasing 
GPU/ASIC-to-optical module ratio; 3) We expect that TPU for 2027 will be 8.9m and Google fully 
transitions to large -scale clusters in 2026 and the scale -up layer will adopt optical interconnects. In 
addition, as we expect that CPO/NPO will start since 2H27, represents a pure incremental opportunity 
for optical interconnects supply chain, benefiting FAU, laser, shuffle box, connector and OE players, 
including LITE, GLW, TSEM, Sumitomo and Browave. We also like Marvell and SMTC, for the 
TIA/Driver used in NPO OE.  
Scale-out bandwidth increase driving optical-module deployment 
For NVIDIA, each Rubin GPU is equipped with two CX9 NIC chips, doubling the scale-out bandwidth 
compared with Blackwell. We further estimate that Rubin Ultra will adopt four CX9 chips per GPU, 
leading to another 2 times increase in scale-out bandwidth, continuing NVIDIA’s consistent bandwidth-
doubling trajectory. Accordingly, we expect the ratio of 1.6T optical modules has risen from 1:2.5 to 
1:5 in Rubin. From CoWoS perspective, we expect 5m/2m Blackwell/Rubin and 20m 1.6T in 2026. 
For Google, we believe that Goo gle fully transition to large -scale clusters in 2026 and the scale -up 
layer will adopt optical interconnects. We believe that in the scale-up domain, the ratio of TPU to 1.6T 
optical modules is approximately 1:1.5; In the scale-out domain, the ratio is around 1:2.5, suggesting 
the overall TPU-to-optics ratio is approximately 1:4 (1.6T equivalent).

Nvidia/CSPs’ Scale-out/up CPO/NPO  
Within NVIDIA’s next -gen NVL576 and broader CSPs’ AI infrastructure plans, CPO and NPO are 
developed side -by-side to expand GP U/ASIC interconnect bandwidth: CPO is the ideal long -term 
design for lower power and latency yet faces headwinds from complicated advanced packaging, and 
yield risks, while NPO could deliver most short -reach optical performance gains with far better 
manufacturability and deployment flexibility, making NPO a reliable near -term scaling path and CPO 
the ultimate end -state architecture. NPO partially removes module -side DSP and shifts signal 
conditioning and equalization to system designs, greatly lifting the value of TIAs and laser drivers that 
take over part of traditional DSP functions; we estimate each 3.2T optical engine contains tens of 
dollars worth of combined TIA and driver chips, a clear improvement over standard pluggable optics. 
Our shipment forecasts at 6.5m/42.4m NPO optical engines in CY27/28.  
Scale-up domain to increase by NVL576 to compensate lower memory: With lower memory spec, we 
expect Nvidia to increase the scale -up domain by optics to enhance performance. According to 
Nvidia’s GTC 2026, th e optical scale -up fabric allows Nvidia to expand Rubin Ultra to an NVL576 
scale-up. Regarding CPO and NPO which are being parallelly developed, we expect NPO to be a 
high reliability scaling path for NPO. We now expect its scale -up to adopt NPO in Rubin U ltra and 
Feynman platform and estimate OE shipments of Nvidia’s platform to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028.

### Page 25

Sector Report | Technology

Nvidia’s NPO OE to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028 
For Nvidia, we now expect its scale up to adopt NPO in Rubin Ultra and Feynman platform and 
estimate OE  shipments of Nvidia’s platform to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028, based on full NPO 
adoption at NVL576 launch given NPO maturity. Coupled with other CSP’s platform, we estimate total 
OE market to be 6.5m/42.4m in 2027/2028.  
TIA/Driver to benefit from NPO 
NPO partially removes module -side DSP and shifts signal conditioning and equalization to system 
designs, greatly lifting the value of TIAs and laser drivers that take over part of traditional DSP 
functions; we estimate each 3.2T optical engine contains tens o f dollars worth of combined TIA and 
driver chips, a clear improvement over standard pluggable optics.  
A key architectural shift under NPO is the partial removal of DSP functionality at the module level, with 
signal conditioning and equalization increasing ly redistributed into system -level design. This 
significantly elevates the importance of TIA (transimpedance amplifier) and laser driver components, 
which effectively replace a portion of traditional DSP value. We estimate that each 3.2T optical engine 
will embed high tens of dollars of combined TIA and driver content, representing a meaningful step -
up versus traditional pluggable optics architectures.  
From a competitive standpoint, we expect Marvell to remain the primary beneficiary of both NPO OE 
given i ts stronger positioning high speed TIA/Driver. Semtech is well positioned as a secondary 
beneficiary through its exposure to high-speed analog signal chain components, particularly TIAs and 
laser drivers, which scale directly with optical engine proliferat ion under NPO. We forecast 
6.5mn/42.4mn NPO OE shipments in CY27/28. Based on our estimated front-end content per OE and 
a 30% market share assumption for Semtech, we estimate its NPO revenue of $315/936mn. 
Stock Implication 
Generally speaking, scale-up CPO/NPO is an incremental opportunity for optical interconnect supply 
chain, as it aims to replace copper and has no impact on scale -out side. Additionally, scale up and 
scale out CPO solution share the similar suppliers. That said, we expect the key benefic iaries to be 
components such as FAU, CW laser, ELSFP, shuffle, PIC, TIA and Driver. We: 1) prefer Lumentum/ 
COHR due to the demand upside for CW laser, and also like local cw laser players; 2) expect Browave 
(3163 TT) to benefit, given the sizable contribu tion of CPO’s shuffle box considering ~55% market 
share, $5-6k ASP and a volume ramp in 2H26; 3) we also like TSEM, given CSP preferring NPO 
solution for their ASICs scale up interconnection. 4) expect GLW to benefit, given the second supplier 
of shuffle b ox and main supplier of MMC connector/fiber. 5) Marvell/Semtech will also benefit from 
CSP/Nvidia’s NPO solution, since TIA and driver will upgrade in the NPO Optical Engine.

### Page 28

Sector Report | Technology

Google’s 6D Torus & Beneficiaries.  
We expect Google will migrate its TPU interconnect from 3D to 6D torus, re -organizing the 64 -chip 
elemental block from 4× 4× 4 to 2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2. External optical links per block rise from 96 to 384, with 
optical ports per chip scaling increase from 1:1.5 to 1:6, driving 4x coherent lite transceivers and OCS 
demand. The trajectory aligns with NVIDIA's CPO/NPO move. Both are interconnect scaling that 
expands direct chip-to-chip bandwidth and reduces hop count, with TPU via a 6D torus and NVIDIA 
via CPO/NPO for Scale-up. 
Google’s migration from 3D  to 6D Torus: We expect Google to migrate its TPU interconnect from a 
3D to a 6D torus topology, re-organizing the elemental 64-chip building block from a 4× 4× 4 3D Torus 
cube into a 2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2 hyper-cube. In the current 3D design, each chip connects to 6 nearest 
neighbors across X/Y/Z axes and the cube exposes 16 optical ICI links on each of its 6 faces, totaling 
96 external optical links per cube (~1.5 optical ports per chip). Under the 6D design, each dimension 
has 64 external optical links (2⁶ chips, on e outward link per chip per dimension), for a total of 6 ×  64 
= 384 external optical links (~6 optical ports per chip). The per-chip optical port count thus scales from 
1.5 to 6. Combined with Scale -out at an estimated 1:2.5, this brings the aggregate TPU -to-port ratio 
to 1:8.5. 
Lower latency and closer to non -blocking Scale-up: For a 4,096 -chip superpod, a 3D torus (4× 4× 4) 
carries a worst-case diameter of 24 hops, whereas a 6D torus (2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2) halves that to 12 hops 
(2× 6), directly compressing collective-operation and latency. Higher dimensionality also spreads traffic 
across 6 orthogonal paths, improving bisection bandwidth and moving closer to non-blocking chip-to-
chip communication. Critically, shortening each edge from 4 to 2 nodes eliminates deep mu lti-hop 
electrical paths inside the block: the architecture deliberately reduces intra -cube copper 
communication and pushes a far larger share of traffic onto optical fiber. 
Key beneficiaries: The exact TPU generation for the adoption of 6D torus and initi al penetration rate 
remain unclear. That said, whether NVIDIA moves toward CPO/NPO or Google expands from 3D to 
6D, both are Scale -up strategies aimed at enabling greater inter -chip communication. Key 
beneficiaries include the 2.4T Coherent Lite module and the OCS supply chain, such as MEMS-based 
optical switch vendors, PIC/laser/DSP suppliers, and fiber/cabling vendors supporting the larger 
reconfigurable lightwave fabric.

Risks 
1) Alternative technologies; 2) AI demand and capex slowdown; 3) IT demand slowdown; 4) product 
delays.

Are investors rational?
The stock market strength and resilience over the last 3 months appears to be a rational response to strong
earnings and only limited signs that higher energy prices are materially affecting broad corporate profits.
Our dbLumina gauge indicates investors have been irrational during market

drops and periods of geopolitical tensions What does it mean to be ‘rational’?
Risk-off periods:
The tariff fears in 2025 and then the Iran
war in 2026 were key buying opportunities
in equity markets. Yet, the overwhelming
fear exhibited by investors in these periods
largely meant they sold off risk assets in an
emotional and irrational response.
Risk-on periods:
It can be rational to buy into a rising
market. Our indicator suggests this is what
happened in Q3 as earnings growth

continued to be strong.
Fear Indicators:
Sharp, disproportionate reactions to
threats, widespread sell-offs on
announcements, panic, flight to safety
beyond what might be fundamentally
warranted.

### Page 9

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 1
A reallocation of global sovereign reserve assets is accelerating, spurred by
geopolitical uncertainty and the fear of trade and financial sanctions. Notably,
central banks are repatriating their physical assets, such as the Dutch and French

central banks which have recently moved gold reserves out of New York.
Furthermore, a World Gold Council survey reveals that 45% of global central banks
plan to increase their gold reserves over the next 12 months.
What the market underestimates: The market views the recent surges in gold prices
as temporary, cyclical safe-haven flows. In reality, sovereign institutions are
structurally preparing for a more fragmented global trade system where tariff

policies and sanctions may make dollar-clearing assets more difficult.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Interest rates: At a time when the "world is awash in debt", long-term interest rates
of some countries may be forced structurally higher to attract alternative private
capital.
Inflation: Unexpected dollar depreciation may increase the cost of imported goods
Equity Prices: High-growth US equity valuations, which are highly sensitive to the

long-term risk-free rate, may face multiple compression if bond yields are pushed
higher.
The market currently prices in a material, long-term GDP boost from AI-driven
productivity gains. However, some research from the IMF suggests that over-reliance
on standardised LLMs can produce a high degree of "sameness" and uniformity in
cognitive output. Furthermore, escalating intellectual property and copyright
litigation threaten to bifurcate the quality of AI models based strictly on developers'

licensing budgets.
What the market underestimates: Some investors assume that the exponential
curve of AI hardware capabilities translates directly into exponential economic
output. But there is a risk that if AI tools merely standardise corporate processes
rather than innovate them, and legal barriers restrict access to premium data, the
productivity boom may be less aggressive than expected.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
GDP growth: Structural growth ceiling if AI fails to deliver the multi-percentage

point boost to global productivity required to offset aging demographics.
Equity prices: A downward revaluation of the technology and software sector if
massive AI infrastructure expenditures fail to generate revenue according to existing
expectations.
Geopolitical fragmentation of global reserve assets The AI “sameness“ trap and the productivity stagnation1 2
8

### Page 10

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 2
Advanced economies are facing a dual demographic crisis: rapidly aging populations
and underfunded pension systems. This is forcing some older people to remain in or
re-enter the workforce out of financial necessity rather than choice.

What the market underestimates: Lower unemployment rates are celebrated as a
sign of economic strength. However, in structurally ageing societies like Japan and
parts of Europe, the growth of the labour force is increasingly driven by part-time,
older workers. Furthermore, G20 nations lack adequate, cohesive policy frameworks
to address these long-term demographic fiscal drains.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: An older workforce working out of necessity has less discretionary

spending power, potentially locking economies into persistent, structural demand-
side deflationary forces, offset by supply-side wage pressures in highly skilled
sectors.
GDP growth: Labour productivity growth that is lower than it might otherwise be as
the median age of the workforce climbs, limiting long-term potential GDP.
The geopolitical battleground is focussed on energy blockades (such as the Strait of
Hormuz) but underestimates the weaponisation of critical minerals. China currently

holds ‘escalation dominance’ over rare-earth mineral supply chains. The emerging
risk is the proactive restriction of these minerals to Western firms, such as recent
bans on supplying rare-earth inputs to some US firms.
What the market underestimates: The market treats rare-earth supply constraints as
temporary trade spat negotiation tactics. In reality, as Western nations aggressively
push for reshoring and friend-shoring, China is increasingly using its processing

monopoly as a long-term strategic blockade to try to slow Western high-tech,
defence and the energy transition.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: Upwards pressure on inflation as the cost of manufacturing electric
vehicles, semiconductors, and advanced electronics surges due to supply blockades
and the high capital cost of rebuilding localised processing facilities.
Interest rates: Central banks may be forced to keep policy rates higher to combat

the persistent supply-side inflation generated by supply-chain fragmentation.
The fragmentation of the EU’s political centre Critical mineral isolationism and ‘green’ supply chain blockades3 4
9

### Page 11

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 1
Our dbLumina AI model helps us identify the key emerging themes that have the potential to
most impact markets and economies in the short term
While the previous focus was on the AI hardware squeeze and
commercial competition, the narrative is shifting towards AI's role as a

critical component of national security and geopolitical leverage.
— AI access: We see this in President Trump's framing of AI development as a
geopolitical race ("whoever wins AI wins").
— AI as a bargaining chip: Several examples over the last 12 months including the
2025 deal whereby AMD agreed to pay 15% of sales from certain AI chips to the
US government in exchange for an export license. Even if these deals change, the
trajectory is one where access to AI technology is tied to national economic and

security interests.
— Direct military & security applications: Several examples including, cyberattacks
on critical infrastructure (such as a UK power plant), the use of AI in developing
"exceptional" phages to fight E.coli, and the growing field of AI security
discussions.
Outlook: Expect more government intervention, the use of AI technology access as a
tool in diplomatic negotiations, and a greater focus on the dual-use nature of AI in
both commercial and military contexts. This will likely create regulatory and

operational uncertainty for tech firms.
The trend of reshoring is evolving into a more proactive diplomatic
strategy where nations are forming new, often transactional, alliances
to navigate a world less dominated by a single superpower.
— Proactive alliance building: Canadian Prime Minister Carney has been front and
centre making his first international trip to France and the UK in an effort to
diversify away from traditional reliance on the US. That follows other key

meetings between Japan and India, and more.
— Bypassing traditional powers: Greenland signalled its willingness to turn to
China for investment if Western countries do not provide support, reinforcing the
leverage of some smaller nations.
— Shifting security arrangements: The US pushed for a ‘reassurance force’ in
Ukraine, while French President Macron hosted a "coalition of the willing" to
discuss Europe's defence without US guarantees. Further examples include the

discussion of a Franco-German "nuclear sharing" arrangement.
Outlook: The assumptions of a bipolar world order is giving way to a more complex,
multi-nodal system. This will create greater emphasis on regional blocs and issue-
specific coalitions. The stability of traditional alliances like NATO and the G7 remain
deeply uncertain.
AI’s impact on geopolitics and national security The fracturing of multilateralism and the rise of "Middle Power Countries" 1 2

10

### Page 12

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 2
These four key themes dbLumina identifies are sometimes ‘known’ by investors, however, they may be underestimated by the market,
particularly when there are some potentially series longer-term impacts
Previous talk of “sovereign fiscal fragility” has now manifested itself

into a severe market reaction. Although fears around government debt
and deficits pop up from time to time, the magnitude of September’s
bond sell-off has been compounded by oil price concerns
— Accelerating sovereign sell-off: Concerns about inflation and debt sustainability
are not new but the peaks in bond yields are. Yields on 10-year Treasuries, bunds,
and gilts all rose sharply, with bunds hitting their highest level since 2009.
— Fiscal policy as a direct market catalyst: The market is increasingly sensitive to

fiscal policy announcements. For example, in the UK postponed tax rises, led to a
spike in gilt yields. Similarly, France's political turmoil and concerns over its
budget deficit caused the Franco-German yield spread to widen significantly.
— The “awash in debt" narrative: Treasury Secretary Bessent's comment at the G20
that "The world is awash in debt" signalled that the issue is increasingly top-of-
mind for policymakers.
Outlook: Markets will be increasingly quick to punish any perceived fiscal

irresponsibility. Expect higher borrowing costs, currency volatility, and increased risk
of financial instability, especially for countries with high debt levels.
The strain in the US consumer and labour markets is now diverging
sharply from the health of large corporations, reinforcing the K-shaped
economy.
— Strong corporate performance: Earnings season for Q2 showed remarkably solid
global growth, and the resilience of large corporations was a recurring theme

that helped support equity markets despite other headwinds.
— Weak consumer & labour signals: Economic reports have showed some signs of
a weakening labour market, falling consumer confidence, and stress among low-
income Americans. The New York Fed's survey also showed households expect a
worse financial situation.
— Policy and market disconnect: While strong corporate performance depends on
a robust economy, the underlying consumer weakness is concerning. Expect the

market to continue its twitchy reaction to data, as investors struggle to reconcile
these conflicting signals.
Outlook: This theme is potentially the most consequential for the domestic US
economy. If corporate profits and equity markets "detach" from the real economy as
consumers perceive it, it could lead to significant social and political instability,
making economic policy even more difficult to navigate.
The return of global bond vigilantes The bifurcation of economies: corporate resilience vs. consumer strain3 4

## Document 340: Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

23 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
NEXT GENERATION EU: THE PROGRAMME IS COMING TO AN END, BUT ITS EFFECTS ON ECONOMIC ACTIVITY WILL
ENDURE
Guillaume Derrien
A pillar of the NextGenerationEU programme, the Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) will expire on December 31, 2026. As of the
end of August 2026, slightly more than three-quarters of the total budget had been disbursed (EUR 440 billion out of EUR 573 billion).
Disbursing the remaining quarter (EUR 133 billion) by the end of the year will require a significant increase compared with the histo-

rical pace (EUR 90 billion per year). The goal of channeling these funds toward productive investment appears to have been achieved.
Tangible results for investment in Europe
According to Eurostat, public spending co-financed by the RRF totaled EUR 310 billion between 2020 and 2025. Nearly three-quarters (74%)
consisted of capital expenditures, including EUR 106 billion in direct public investment and EUR 122 billion in capital transfers to the private

sector (such as grants). This spending supported investment by businesses and households.
The list of major programmes funded by the RFF, published by the European Commission 1, shows that, within this scope, nearly two-thirds of
the funding went to transportation (primarily rail), energy infrastructure, and digital infrastructure (see Chart 1).
Another notable point: capital expenditures co-financed by the RRF increased significantly, reaching 0.4% of GDP for the European Union as a

whole in 2025 (see Chart 2). There are significant disparities among Member States, primarily due to differences in the amounts allocated to
them. Croatia (1.9%), Greece (1.2%), Italy (1%), and Spain (0.7%) received significant support, in contrast to France and Germany (0.1%). These
figures are consistent with the IMF’s estimate that the RRF added approximately 0.3 percentage points (pp) to EU growth in 2025 (more than
1 pp in Greece and Croatia, around 0.8 pp in Italy, and 0.5 pp in Spain) 2. Furthermore, as the first major joint European borrowing initiative, the

RRF made it possible to finance investment without increasing national budget deficits, giving several countries (Spain, Italy, Greece, Portugal)
valuable breathing room to consolidate their public finances.
1 Recovery and Resilience Scoreboard
2 The EU’s Recovery and Resilience Facility: Experience and Lessons for the Next Multiannual Financial Framework, WP/26/114, June 2026
SOURCE: EUROPEAN COMMISSION, Recovery and Resilience Scorecard

Chart of the Week / 23 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
What impact on EU’s economic activity can we expect once this programme ends?
At first glance, effects will be limited. The end of the programme is expected to weigh on both public and private investment. However, the
strengthening of economic activity that we anticipate (see details in our latest Ecoperspectives – Q3 2026) rests primarily on German public

investment programmes and structural drivers (investment in AI, defence spending).
The ECB makes the same point in its September macroeconomic projections 3. It emphasises that the end of the RRF is expected to weigh
on public investment in 2027, while it anticipates an acceleration in business investment. Furthermore, RRF funds are being absorbed on
the ground only gradually. Since most disbursements are concentrated toward the end of the programme, the effects of the RRF will extend

beyond 2026.
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

Despite risk, agricultural export prices to remain elevated.
• Ongoing Middle East tensions are increasing volatility and raising energy, input
and shipping costs, while broadly supporting prices for agricultural products.

Impacts vary depending on idiosyncratic supply and demand conditions.
• Risks are exacerbated by the impact of a now confirmed super-sized El Niño,
which could slice off up to 1ppt of New Zealand’s GDP should on-farm
production of both milk and meat contract significantly.
• We have revised up our farmgate milk price forecast for this season by 25c to
$9.75/kg, with milk powder prices holding up better than we expected. El Niño
presents a clear upside risk to this, as it does not appear to have been reflected

in GDT auction prices to date.
• Meanwhile average New Zealand beef export prices should remain elevated over
the coming year, as lean trimming prices reverse recent declines. Sheepmeat
prices are set to ease on rising Australian supply, but remain at elevated levels.
• Kiwifruit and apple export prices are expected to remain elevated, supported
by strong demand for premium varieties, although increasing supply is likely to
keep prices largely flat rather than drive further gains.

• Export prices for New Zealand logs are set to remain broadly stable to slightly
firmer, supported by lower log supply and balanced Chinese inventories.

Source: Macrobond, Stats NZ, Westpac
%yr Index
La Niña
El Niño
Farming is well placed to navigate impacts of a strong El Niño.
• Scientists have now confirmed that the current El Niño event will be the

strongest on record. Temperatures in the Pacific Ocean are now 3.05C above the
average, exceeding the record set in 2015.
• A supersized El Niño increases the probability of drier weather in eastern regions
of New Zealand in coming months, with wetter conditions likelier in the west and
southern areas of the country.
• The potential impact on GDP could be significant. Our previous research has
shown a severe drought could slice off as much as 1ppt from economic growth.

• Much of that is will reflect lower agriculture production, particularly in dairy,
sheep and beef farms. Increased on-farm costs and processing disruptions are
also likely.
• A 1% loss in GDP is likely an upper end estimate of the El Niño impact. A key
consideration is soil moisture levels are currently high, implying some resilience
to future drier conditions.
• Farming practices have become more resilient to prolonged dry periods.
Irrigation is more widely used than in past dry periods and snowmelt levels

appear robust, which should be of particular benefit to Canterbury farms.

EL NIÑO

Conflict-related disruptions could push input costs higher than expected.
• Farm input costs are on the rise reflecting two key drivers: fuel and
fertiliser costs.
• Diesel and petrol prices have increased by about 50% compared to the same
period in 2025. While average prices fell in the September quarter, they closed

the quarter at an elevated level as tensions between the US and Iran increased
and broadened to encompass Saudi Arabia and the Yemeni Houthis.
• Fertiliser prices are also significantly higher. While the fertiliser market has
stabilised after a period of extreme volatility earlier this year, prices remain
highly sensitive to developments in the Middle East.
• Nitrogen (urea) prices have fallen substantially from a mid-year spike, with an
pick up in Chinese exports now boosting supply. These prices though remain

vulnerable to supply disruptions.
• By contrast, phosphate and other sulphur-based fertiliser prices are tracking
higher and remain at elevated levels, as supply constraints, trade restrictions
and disruptions to shipping offset the impact of softening demand in some
regions of the world.

Sources: Fonterra, Stats NZ, Westpac Westpac
forecasts
Farmgate milk price forecast revised up on strong protein demand.
• We have revised up our farmgate milk price forecast for this season by 25c to
$9.75/kg. This is above Fonterra’s forecast midpoint of $9.50/kg (range of $8.50-

10.50), but lower than futures market pricing of just over $10/kg.
• We have also opened our forecast for the 2027/28 season at $9.50/kg. Note that
there is a wide margin of uncertainty around the forecast at this stage, and we
will review as the next season approaches.
• Dairy prices at auction have generally held up better than our expectations,
although the performance has varied across products. Trends in global demand
are shifting away from fats and towards proteins, which has boosted demand

and prices for skim milk powder in particular.
• Our forecasts do not include an assumption about the impact of El Niño, which
presents a clear upside risk to prices and a downside risk to volumes. While the
futures market is pricing a strong lift in milk powder prices over the New Zealand
summer, there has been no apparent urgency to buy at the GDT auction.
Fonterra is forecasting a 2% lift in milk volumes over the rest of this season, on
top of what was already a strong increase last season.

• Global milk production also remains ample, although it has slowed in Europe
in recent months. El Niño is less likely to affect conditions among the major
producers in the Northern Hemisphere.

Average beef export prices
US Beef (Imp 95 CL) prices, 1-month advanced
US$ c/lb US$ c/lb
Source: AgriHQ, Stats NZ
Export beef prices to remain elevated over the next year.
• Beef export prices remain well supported but have softened in recent
months. The weakness has been concentrated in lean beef trimming prices,

which have eased since April as high US prices attracted additional imports,
particularly from South America, while tariff-free quotas temporarily boosted
product availability.
• In contrast, premium beef prices have remained firm, supported by tight global
supplies of high-quality beef, constrained US cattle numbers, and resilient
demand in higher-end retail and foodservice channels.
• We expect that lean trimming prices will reverse recent declines in coming

months as temporary increases in supply begin to fade and underlying
shortages, including the historically small US beef herd, re-emerge.
• In 2027, lean trimming prices should remain elevated but broadly flat as
strong US demand and a small US cattle herd are largely offset by rising global
production. From 2028 onwards, expanding global beef production gradually
eases market conditions, putting pressure on prices.
• Meanwhile, favourable supply/demand fundamentals to support premium beef

prices in 2027, with expanding global production to weigh on prices from 2028.
• Overall, beef export prices are likely to remain high, although both nominal and
real prices are expected to moderate over the medium term.

Source: AgriHQ, Stats NZ
TWI weighted price
Lamb export prices to remain firm, but off recent highs.
• NZ sheepmeat export prices remain near record highs, supported by
constrained global supply and resilient demand from premium markets in

Europe, the UK and North America. Export values rose strongly through 2025/26
despite only modest growth in export volumes, highlighting the strength of
underlying prices.
• Global supply remains tight due to declining sheep flocks and lower slaughter
numbers in New Zealand, the UK and parts of Europe. However, rising Australian
production and exports are expected to gradually lift global supply and increase
competition in key export markets, limiting further price gains.

• Meanwhile, stronger demand from the UK, EU and North America has improved
export returns and reduced New Zealand's reliance on China, where economic
weakness has made consumers more price sensitive and encouraged a shift
towards cheaper proteins and cuts.
• The outlook remains positive but more balanced. Tight supply should keep
prices above historical averages over the coming year, although rising Australian
exports, softer demand growth, affordability pressures, signs of buyer

resistance and (eventually) a stronger NZ dollar could see prices ease modestly
from current elevated levels into 2027.

Capacity is catching up with demand, limiting further price gains.
• NZ kiwifruit export prices have pushed to record highs, supported by strong
demand in North America, Europe, Japan and Korea, as well as continued

consumer preference for premium varieties such as SunGold.
• Export prices continue to edge higher even as a record crop and larger fruit
volumes are helping to reduce market tightness. Zespri notes increased
competition from summer fruit, pricing pressure in parts of Asia and a larger-
than-expected crop.
• Kiwifruit export prices are set to remain high but mostly flat over the next
year, as strong demand for premium varieties such as SunGold and RubyRed is

increasingly offset by rising production and price pressures in Asia.
• Apple export prices are also at record highs, supported by strong demand in
Asia, North America and India, alongside a continued shift towards higher-value
cultivars and premium varieties.
• These factors should keep export prices elevated over the next 12 months,
supporting grower revenues and profitability.
• However, further gains could be limited. Rising New Zealand production,

recovering supplies from key growing regions and larger global apple crops are
expected to increase supply availability, reducing market tightness and keeping
prices broadly stable.

Monthly
Source: AgriHQ
Index IndexLog prices look moribund.
• China remains the dominant market for New Zealand logs, with prices highly
sensitive to construction activity, sawmill demand and daily log offtake. In that
regard, weak housing and furniture demand continue to weigh on prices.

• Chinese softwood log inventories are estimated at around 2.2 to 2.5m m³,
equivalent to roughly 40 to 45 days of inventory cover at current offtake rates.
This suggests inventory levels are broadly balanced and exerting little pressure
on log prices.
• At the same time, reduced New Zealand log supply, including lower harvesting
volumes and the wind-down of salvage logging, is providing some support to
prices. Freight costs also remain an important driver of at-wharf-gate returns,

with currently high shipping costs crimping returns to forest owners.
• NZ log export prices are expected to remain broadly stable to modestly firmer
over the next 12 months, supported by lower domestic supply, balanced Chinese
inventory levels and tentative signs of stabilisation in log demand.
• However, any recovery is likely to be gradual, with weakness in China's property
sector, uncertainty around shipping costs and softer global growth continuing to

constrain demand and cap upside potential.

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國就業回穩支撐景氣韌性，惟通膨壓力與貿易逆差擴大增添政策不確定性 
經濟 
⚫ 8 月美國非農就業增加 16.2 萬人，優於前月及近一年平均，失業率維持
4.1%，就業市場呈現溫和增長，整體表現仍具韌性。新增就業以餐飲服
務、地方政府教育及製造業為主，惟資訊業仍減少人力。 
⚫ 7 月美國職位空缺數增至 727.1 萬個 ， 職缺率由4.3%升至 4.4%； 惟聘僱
及總離職人數分別減少 27.8 萬人及 26.5 萬人，其中主動離職人數減少
15.7 萬人，裁員與解僱人數減少11.9 萬人。整體來看，企業用人需求尚
屬穩健，但招募及勞工流動動能略有降溫，勞動市場供需仍維持平衡。 
⚫ 8 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數為 54.6，雖較上月下降 1.0 點，但
已連續八個月維持擴張。新訂單、就業、未完成訂單及進口指數回落，
顯示需求動能有所放緩 ； 惟生產與出口持續擴張 ， 供應商交貨時間延長 ，
原物料價格仍處高檔，整體製造業活動仍維持擴張態勢。 
⚫ 8 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數由 54.1 升至 55.4，連續第 26 個月
維持擴張。商業活動與新訂單指數分別升至 61.7 及 60.9，反映服務業
需求仍具支撐；惟雇傭指數連續兩個月低於 50，企業聘僱態度仍較審
慎。此外，價格指數升至 72.6，創 2022 年 8 月以來新高，通膨與成本
壓力依然偏高。 
⚫ 7 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 886 億美元，較上月增加 24.4%，
主因出口減少 2.1%、進口增加 2.8%。出口下滑主要受原油及非貨幣黃
金等工業原料出口減少影響；進口則受電腦、半導體等資本財需求增加
帶動。累計前 7 月貿易逆差較去年同期縮減 29.6%，顯示美國對外貿易
失衡情況明顯改善。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 美國於 8 月 30 日以伊朗革命衛隊疑似準備在荷姆茲海峽部署水雷、威
脅商業航運安全為由，對伊朗拉拉克島軍事設施發動精準打擊。伊朗隨
後向約旦境內美軍基地發射飛彈及無人機報復，區域緊張情勢明顯升
溫。由於荷姆茲海峽為全球重要原油運輸樞紐，市場對能源供應與航運
安全的疑慮加深，帶動國際油價及避險需求走高，並增添全球通膨及金
融市場不確定性。 
短評 ⚫ 8 月非農就業優於預期 ， 失業率維持低檔 ， 勞動市場尚未明顯轉弱 ；7 月

管道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，進而提高核心通膨黏性。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.9.7 
一、 美國—就業回穩支撐景氣韌性，惟通膨壓力與貿易逆差擴大增添政策不確
定性 
2026 年 8 月美國就業市場呈現回穩跡象，非農就業人數較上月增加 16.2 萬
人，不僅高於上月修正後的 2.1 萬人，也明顯優於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 3.1
萬人的水準；失業率則維持於 4.1%，失業人數約 703.1 萬人，顯示勞動市場整體
尚未明顯轉弱。從產業表現來看，新增就業主要集中於餐飲服務業增加 5.9 萬人
及地方政府教育部門增加 4.2 萬人 ， 製造業亦增加1.6 萬人 ， 持續支撐就業成長 ；
醫療保健業增加 1.3 萬人，雖維持擴張，但增幅低於過去一年平均水準。另一方
面 ， 資訊業減少2.3 萬人 ， 為主要拖累來源 ， 反映部分產業仍處於人力調整階段 。
勞動市場結構方面，勞動參與率回升至 61.6%，較前月改善，但仍較今年 1 月低
0.5 個百分點；就業人口比率則上升至 59.1%。薪資與工時方面，民營部門平均每
週工時升至 34.4 小時，平均時薪增至 37.75 美元，月增 0.3%、年增 3.1%，薪資
成長保持溫和。整體而言，8 月就業數據顯示美國勞動市場在前月疲弱後回穩，
且前兩個月數據上修，反映就業市場仍具韌性。強勁就業報告公布後，美債殖利
率走升，市場對聯準會 9 月維持偏緊政策的預期升溫，升息可能性亦有所提高。 
2026 年 7 月美國職位空缺數為 727.1 萬個，較上月增加 8.9 萬個，職缺率由
4.3%升至 4.4%；聘僱人數為 505.4 萬人，較上月減少 27.8 萬人，聘僱率由 3.4%
降至 3.2%；總離職人數為 507.2 萬人，較上月減少 26.5 萬人，離職率亦由 3.4%
降至 3.2%，顯示勞動市場流動性有所降溫。就離職結構觀察，主動離職人數為
305.6 萬人，較上月減少 15.7 萬人；裁員與解僱人數為 166.6 萬人，較上月減少
11.9 萬人 。 產業別方面 ， 職位空缺增加主要集中於耐久財製造業(增加 7.6 萬個)；
聘僱人數則以專業及商業服務業減少 18.8 萬人最為明顯 ； 主動離職人數以住宿及
餐飲服務業減少 8.6 萬人最多。整體而言，7 月美國職缺小幅增加，顯示企業用
人需求仍具支撐，惟聘僱及離職規模同步縮減，反映勞動市場流動性趨緩；加以
主動離職率及裁員率雙雙下降，顯示勞工轉職意願與企業人力調整均趨於審慎，
勞動市場供需大致維持平衡。 
2026 年 8 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)為 54.6，較

用相關內容。

5

上月的 55.6 下降 1.0 點，惟已連續第八個月維持在 50 以上擴張區間，顯示美國
製造業景氣持續擴張。細項觀察，新訂單指數由56.7 降至 53.7，雖連續第八個月
處於擴張區間，但需求動能較前月放緩；生產指數由58.5 微降至 58.3，連續第十
個月擴張，產出活動維持穩健；就業指數由52.8 降至 51.2，仍處擴張區間，反映
企業聘僱需求持續增加。供應商交貨指數由58.9 升至 59.3，供應商交貨時間持續
延長；庫存指數則由 51.2 降至 50.6，企業仍持續累積庫存，但備貨動能較前月趨
緩。此外，價格指數維持71.1 不變，原物料價格仍處相對高檔；新出口訂單指數
由 53.0 微升至 53.2， 連續第二個月維持擴張 ； 惟未完成訂單指數由55.0 降至 51.8，
進口指數亦由 55.7 降至 52.5，顯示部分需求與投入動能有所降溫。整體而言，8
月美國製造業活動雖仍維持擴張，但擴張力道較前月略有減弱，新訂單、就業、
未完成訂單及進口等指標同步回落，反映需求成長動能趨緩；另一方面，供應商
交貨時間持續延長、原物料價格維持高檔，加以中東地緣政治衝突、關稅政策及
供應鏈不確定性仍存，可能持續對製造業活動形成壓力。 
2026 年 8 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI) 升至 55.4，較上
月 54.1 上升 1.3 個百分點，連續第26 個月維持在 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近12
個月平均值 53.7，顯示美國服務業景氣持續穩健擴張。商業活動指數由59.1 升至
61.7，創 2022 年 11 月以來最高水準；新訂單指數由 57.2 升至 60.9，連續 15 個
月維持擴張，創 2023 年 2 月以來新高，顯示需求動能進一步增強。勞動市場方
面，雇傭指數由 47.4 微升至 47.8，但已連續兩個月處於收縮區間，反映企業聘僱
態度仍偏審慎。價格指數由 70.3 升至 72.6，創 2022 年 8 月以來最高水準，顯示
通膨與成本壓力依舊偏高。供應鏈方面，供應商交期指數由52.8 降至 51.3，雖連
續 21 個月高於 50，但已連續第四個月改善；庫存指數則由 51.4 升至 56.7，顯示
企業備貨意願增強。此外，新出口訂單指數及進口指數均升至 56.3，反映外部需
求與採購活動同步回溫。整體而言，8 月美國服務業景氣擴張力道進一步增強，
商業活動與新訂單表現亮眼，惟就業指數仍低於榮枯線，加上企業持續關注關稅
政策及中東衝突對供應鏈與成本的影響，後續仍須觀察通膨與勞動市場發展。 
2026 年 7 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 886 億美元 ， 較上月增加174 億
美元，增幅 24.4%。當月出口 3,107 億美元，較上月減少 66 億美元(-2.1%)；進口
3,993 億美元，較上月增加 108 億美元(+2.8%)。其中，商品貿易逆差擴大至1,196
億美元，較上月增加 17.3%；服務貿易順差則增至 310 億美元，增幅 0.7%。出口

下滑主要受工業用品及原材料出口減少 87 億美元影響，其中原油及非貨幣黃金
出口降幅較為明顯 ； 進口成長則主要來自資本財進口增加144 億美元 ， 尤以電腦 、
電腦周邊設備及半導體進口增加最為突出。累計 2026 年 1 至 7 月，美國商品及
服務貿易逆差為 4,472 億美元 ， 較2025 年同期縮減 29.6%； 同期出口成長12.0%，
進口則增加 1.9%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡情況較去年同期明顯改善。 
8 月 30 日美軍以伊朗革命衛隊(IRGC)疑似準備於荷姆茲海峽部署水雷 、 威脅
商業航運安全為由，對伊朗拉拉克島(Larak Island) 火箭發射設施發動精準打擊，
為美方逾一個月來首度對伊朗本土目標採取軍事行動。伊朗隨後以飛彈及無人機
攻擊約旦與阿聯酋境內美軍相關設施作為回應，顯示衝突已由雙邊軍事對抗逐步
向區域層面擴散。至 9 月 5 日美國中央司令部(CENTCOM)表示，因伊朗革命衛
隊向兩艘美國海軍艦艇發射彈道飛彈，美軍已對 3 艘伊朗油輪發動攻擊，其中一
艘位於伊朗重要石油出口樞紐哈爾克島(Kharg Island)外海。美方稱未造成人員傷
亡，惟伊朗半官方媒體指出，哈爾克島錨地附近油輪遭 4 枚美軍飛彈擊中，船員
已展開撤離。由於哈爾克島為伊朗最重要的原油出口據點，戰前約九成原油出口
均經由該地，加上荷姆茲海峽為全球關鍵能源運輸通道，本次衝突已明顯升高市
場對油輪航行安全、原油供應穩定性及運輸成本上揚的憂慮，帶動布蘭特原油期
貨於 9 月 4 日收報每桶 96.28 美元，創 7 月 24 日以來新高。整體而言，美伊衝突
已由原先針對軍事設施的交鋒，進一步延伸至能源運輸與油輪目標，若後續報復
與反制行動持續升級，恐加劇中東原油供應中斷風險，推升全球能源價格與通膨
壓力，並提高市場對美國經濟前景及聯準會貨幣政策走向的不確定性。 
二、歐元區—通膨壓力回升，核心通膨走向值得關注 
 歐元區 7 月失業率持平 6.4%，失業人數持平上月水準。其中德國失業人數增
加 1.1 萬人，失業率持平為 4%；法國、義大利失業人數皆下降 1 萬人，失業率分
別持平為 8.3%、5.8%； 西班牙失業人數持平上月 ， 惟因部分勞動力退出勞動市場 ，
使失業率略降為 10.1%。整體而言，失業率維持在 6.3%-6.4%的低點區間波動，
且 8 月 PMI 的就業指數也有所增溫， 顯示勞動市場的韌性可望延續， 並支持消費
需求表現。 
 歐元區 8 月 HICP 通膨上升 0.4 個百分點至 3.3%，其中因燃料價格上揚，能
源通膨上升 4 個百分點至 14.3%，食品通膨則穩定於 1.2%，迄今能源及化肥價格

上漲仍未對食品價格造成明顯影響 ， 惟不排除存在滯後效應 。 核心通膨(不含食品
及能源)則下降 0.1 個百分點至 2.4%， 主要是服務業通膨下降0.3 個百分點至 3%，
反映旅遊相關通膨放緩；非能源工業產品通膨則上升 0.3 個百分點至 1.2%，主因
天氣炎熱，使消暑產品價格上漲，加上 3C 產品漲價的趨勢影響。整體而言，能
源價格雖然強勁回升，但其影響尚未傳導至核心物價，且薪資上漲壓力降溫，減
輕服務通膨上漲壓力 。 不過 ，8 月以來布蘭特原油大致位於每桶 85-95 美元區間 ，
預期年底前仍將對通膨帶來額外壓力，後續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物
流等成本管道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，進而提高核心通膨黏性。 
三、日本—製造業產出穩健成長，消費動能仍具韌性 
7 月零售月增 2.4%，部份反映 6 月受梅雨及豪雨天氣影響後的回升，顯示消
費動能仍具韌性。其中耐久財消費持續受到政策及汰舊換新需求支撐，家電銷售
在 6 月大幅下滑後明顯回升 ， 主要受空調等產品需求帶動 ； 汽車銷售額大致持平，
顯示政府自 4 月推動的稅務優惠措施效果尚未消退。另，非耐久財消費亦呈現全
面改善，服裝銷售受炎熱天氣帶動而大幅回升，一般商品銷售則反映國內消費及
觀光需求同步增強，食品銷售亦維持成長， 惟部分增幅可能並非實際需求增加，
若未來食品通膨進一步升溫，恐壓縮家庭實質購買力及消費信心，進而對後續非
耐久財支出及消費動能形成壓力。 
7 月工業生產月增 0.1%，主要係人工智慧相關需求帶動，科技與非科技產業
產出同步增加，其中半導體製造設備、電子零組件及化學工業表現尤為亮眼。出
貨月增 2.2%，高於生產增幅，而庫存僅增 0.5%、庫存率下降 1.7%，顯示產品去
化情況良好，整體供需維持穩健。展望未來，企業預估 8 月工業生產將大幅成長
6.4%，雖 9 月可能略為回落，但PMI 等前瞻指標仍顯示製造業活動持續擴張，預
計第三季製造業產出可望維持穩健成長。 
四、中國—8 月景氣略為回穩，但前景仍有較多不利因素 
8 月官方製造業 PMI 回升 0.6 點至 49.8，連續兩個月處於微幅衰退區間，新
訂單、出口訂單指數雖分別回升至 50.6、50.1，並帶動生產指數回到 50.4，不過，
庫存指數下降 、 就業指數加速衰退 、 企業展望減弱 ， 皆拖累PMI 指數水準 。 此外 ，
供應鏈交期指數上升至 50.1、投入物價指數回升至 56.6，反映交期拉長及成本加
速上漲，使業者營運承壓；產出物價指數回升 2.6 點至 50.4，售價調整速度依然

較為遲緩，不利於獲利表現。另外，RatingDog 的 8 月製造業 PMI 則回升 0.6 點

官方非製造業 PMI 持平 49，其中建築業活動指數降 0.1 點至 46.9，為連續八
個月處於衰退區間；服務業商務活動指數持平 49.3，非製造業的從業人員指數為
45.4，顯示就業情況仍顯嚴峻，亦較不利於內需表現。另方面，RatingDog 服務業
PMI 增加 1 點至 51.4，較 7 月的低點溫和反彈。相較於官方非製造業 PMI，
RatingDog 服務業因不包含大型不動產開發商而表現較佳，且內需動能有回升跡
象，不過，RatingDog 的產出價格指數跌破 50，為今年首見，反映下游競爭激烈
導致成本難以轉嫁，故雖通膨可望維持低檔，但企業獲利將持續承壓。 
展望未來，G20 峰會將中國龐大貿易順差視為加劇全球經濟失衡的重要因素
之一，國際社會要求中國擴大內需及調整經濟結構的壓力持續升高。此外，中國
仍面臨美國 301 關稅調查、歐盟擴大貿易保護措施等，出口環境仍具不確定性。
未來將關注 9 月 24 日川習會，預計雙方將就關稅措施、出口管制、AI 及先進科
技管制等議題進行協商，若會談結果正向，將有助緩解中美貿易摩擦，降低出口
面臨之政策風險。 
五、印度及東南亞—印度經濟保持強勁、馬來西亞央行利率不變 
印度: 2026 年第二季(2026/27 財年第一季)經濟成長率 （GDP） 年增7.8%，持平於
上季，仍為二十國集團（G20）成長最快國家，成長最快行業依序為:金融服務業
12.1%、製造業 9.2%、公用事業 8.9%、貿易/旅館/運輸/通訊業 8.5%；不過，農業
產出僅增長 3.6%，採礦及採石業則收縮 2.4%。儘管上半年經濟成長表現強勁，
製造業、消費和信貸均展現韌性；不過，油價上漲、印度盧比承壓以及季風降雨
不足等風險持續積累 ， 並透過貿易逆差 、 通膨和企業成本等管道向實體經濟傳導 ，
為經濟前景增添不確定性。 
東南亞:8 月 S&P Global 東協 （ASEAN） 製造業採購經理人指數(PMI)較上月小幅
回落 0.5 點至 52.3，仍保持穩健擴張態勢，其中，新訂單以接近歷史高點的速度
增加，生產活動雖較前月放緩，仍保持穩健；採購活動持續擴張，但聘僱與原物
料庫存小幅下滑，顯示廠商在人力與庫存建置仍偏謹慎，未完成訂單持平前月，
顯示產能壓力依然存在。價格方面，投入成本與產品售價均降至六個月低點，通

膨壓力進一步緩和 ， 供應商交貨時間延遲情況較前月減輕 ； 企業信心進一步增強 ，
未來一年產出預期指數達近四年高點。按個別國家來看，8 月除印尼(49.8)自擴張
轉為收縮外 ， 馬來西亞(50.2)、 泰國(53.8)較上月下滑 ， 新加坡(59.4)、 菲律賓(54.9)、
越南(53.3)均加速擴張。整體而言，在需求穩健、供應鏈改善及價格壓力減輕下，
東協國家製造業可望保持穩健擴張態勢，惟美伊衝突的不確定性仍存，持續推升
通膨預期並干擾供應鏈，同時牽動全球資金流動，仍須留意其對生產及企業獲利
的影響。 
馬來西亞 ：馬來西亞央行 9 月貨幣政策會議 ， 如預期維持基準利率 （OPR）於2.75%
不變。央行指出，儘管全球經濟情勢存在不確定性，得益於強勁的出口、穩健的
就業與內需 ，2026 年上半年經濟成長率(GDP)達 5.7%， 預計2026 年全年可達 5%，
而良好基本面將使 2027 年的經濟成長保持韌性。通膨方面，2026 年前七個月，
平均整體通膨率和核心通膨率分別為 1.8%和 2%，儘管受中東緊張局勢影響，全
球大宗商品價格上漲，但預計物價壓力仍將保持在可控範圍內。整體而言，受益
於全球經濟前景的改善、科技相關出口需求強勁及旅遊業活動增加，為經濟成長
帶來上行潛力，惟地緣政治緊張局勢持續、全球金融環境趨緊以及對金融市場估
值過高的擔憂，仍帶來下行風險。目前的貨幣政策立場與物價持續穩定和經濟可
持續成長的前景相符 ， 未來將密切關注國際局勢變化 、 成本壓力和國內需求狀況 ，
彈性調整政策。

緯穎 6669 TT(電子–電腦及週邊設備) 
GPU、ASIC 並進，27 年獲利創高 
投資與評價 
1. 因缺料影響降低，加上 Trainium3 開始出貨，緯穎 3Q26 營收優於預期
17%，上修獲利 25%至 EPS 43.9 元，3Q26 營收、獲利將同創新高。 
2. 在 ASIC、GPU 貢獻下，4Q26 營收估季增高個位數 ，AI 比重將逾 55~60%，
因 2H26 優於預期，上修今年營收 8%至 1.46 兆元，EPS 140.3 元。 
3. AMD Helios 為緯穎 27 年 AI 強勁動能，加上公司為 Trainium 水冷產品主
供，帶動 27 年稅後獲利 YoY+60%，CB 對股權稀釋僅 1.03%，EPS 估續
創新高達 224.71 元，維持買進評等，目標價 2,400 元。 
重點更新 
3Q26 營收、獲利上修，反映一般伺服器雙位數季增及液冷 ASIC 加入貢獻 
緯穎 3Q26 營收 4,346 億元，QoQ+56%、YoY+63%，優於研究部預期 17%，
主要因缺料影響較預期低，加上 9 月 AWS Trainium3 開始出貨，因本季一般伺
服器占比超越 50%，估毛利率維持 8.53%，稅後獲利上修 25%至 245.95 億元，
YoY+59%至 EPS 43.9 元。預期 3Q26 營收、獲利將同創新高。  
4Q26 AI 比重攀升至 55%以上，ASIC、GPU 同步貢獻，上修今年營收 8% 
研究部估緯穎 4Q26 營收將季增高個位數，AI 部分，ASIC 出貨 Trainium3 液冷
貢獻，ASP 估較氣冷版翻倍成長；此外，GPU 部分，預期 AMD Helios 及 Oracle 
Vera Rubin 皆有少量出貨，加上缺料持續改善，估 AI 比重將超越 55~60%，使
毛利率下滑至 8.2%，稅後獲利 YoY+81%，EPS 44.5 元。因 2H26 狀況優於預
期，上修今年營收 8%至 1.46 兆元 ，YoY+54%，反映下半年 AI 比重攀升至 55%，
毛利率 8.38%，稅後獲利 YoY+54%，EPS 140.34 元。 
27 年營運在 ASIC、GPU 成長增速下，獲利將再創新高 
展望 2027 年，預期 AMD Helios 將為 AI 強勁動能，2027 年 AMD GPU 機櫃估
達五到六千櫃 ，ASP 500 萬美元 ， 加上VR 機櫃貢獻 ， 估使GPU 占比拉升至 30%。
ASIC 部分，我們認為緯穎仍是 Trainium 水冷產品主供，預期 AWS 水冷比重大
幅攀升，估AWS 營收年增四到五成；一般伺服器維持兩成以上增長。綜上，上修
27 年營收 11%至 2.64 兆元，YoY+80.4 %，因 AI 占比估達六到七成，估毛利率
下滑至 7.35%，稅後獲利 YoY+60%，EPS 224.71 元，獲利續創新高。籌資部分，
緯穎將於 11 月完成 150 億元國內可轉債發行，預計在 1Q27~2Q27 分別使用 
80 億元、70.75 億元，對於整體股權稀釋僅 1.03%，影響有限。

達發 ESG 評等

電子製造業 ESG 評等分配表

資料來源:臺灣指數公司，國泰證期研究部整理

達發 6526 TT  ( 電子–半導體 ) 
由網入光，發光發熱 
投資與評價 
1. 達發 3Q26 受惠光通放量與商務耳機出貨暢旺，營收創單季新高；
4Q26 光通持續成長，並同步推進 100G 量產與 OOB 新應用導入，營
運為近五年同期新高。 
2. 單通道 200G SerDes 與 25G PON 持續推進，50G/100G 光通、10G 
PON 與 OOB 新應用同步放量，高價值產品占比提升帶動產品組合優
化，預估 26 年 EPS 20.95 元；27 年 EPS 28.36 元，營運創歷史新高。 
3. 目前達發股價為 783 元，約 28 倍 FY27E EPS，位於近四年本益比參考
區間 20-50 倍中間偏下位置。考量光通進入高速成長期，OOB 帶動
AIDC 新應用，轉型方向明確，初次評等給予買進，以近四年本益比參
考區間平均值 35 倍作為評價基礎，目標價 995 元(35x FY27E PER)。

重點更新 
光通與商務耳機出貨暢旺，預期達發 3Q26 營運創近四年單季新高 
達發 3Q26 光通單通道 50G 受惠替換需求與新增標案持續放量 ， 乙太網受惠
低軌衛星規格升級，2.5G PHY 與交換器晶片出貨成長，藍牙則進入消費旺
季，商務耳機出貨增加，營收季增 10.4%至 69.41 億元，創單季新高。毛利
率受記憶體、NOR Flash 及封測成本上升影響，預估季減 2.1 個百分點至
49.1%，營業費用 23.35 億元 （+2.7% QoQ），營業利益 10.73 億元(+13.8% 
QoQ)；預估稅後淨利季增 11.1%至 9.83 億元，EPS 5.87 元，營運創近四年
單季新高。

光通逐季放量，達發 4Q26 營運為近五年同期新高 
達發 4Q26 進入季節性淡季，惟光通逐季放量，預期單通道 100G 逐步進入
量產，乙太網新一代 10G PHY 亦開始出貨，預估營收 64.40 億元（-7.2% 
QoQ） ，季減幅度小於往年。毛利率受上游成本持續上漲影響，惟受惠高毛利
光通持續放量， 預估毛利率持平為 49.1%，營業費用 22.05 億元（-5.6% 
QoQ），營業利益 9.57 億元（-10.8% QoQ）；預估稅後淨利 8.99 億元（-
8.56% QoQ），EPS 5.36 元，營運為近五年同期新高。

鍾沛寰 / IC 設計 
+886-2-2326-9899#1750/ parisphchung@cathayfut.com.tw

### Page 2

訪談內容 
光通與商務耳機出貨暢旺，預期達發 3Q26 營運創近四年單季新高 
達發 3Q26 藍牙進入消費旺季，商務耳機專案持續出貨，高階應用占比提升，惟上半年提前備貨效應逐步消退，藍
牙音訊出貨成長幅度相對溫和。光通方面，單通道 50G 受惠替換需求與新增標案持續放量，大型 CSP 替換專案亦
於下半年開始出貨，搭配出貨的 TIA 搭載率同步提升；乙太網受惠低軌衛星客戶由 1G 升級至 ASP 較高的 2.5G 
PHY，交換器晶片出貨持續成長；寬頻客戶下單節奏則回歸終端需求，3Q26 營收季增 10.4%至 69.41 億元，創單
季新高。 
毛利率方面，記憶體、NOR Flash 及封測成本上升，而產品漲價尚未反映，惟高毛利光通占比提升，部分抵銷成本
壓力，預估毛利率季減 2.1 個百分點至 49.1%。營業費用 23.35 億元（+2.7% QoQ） ，營收規模擴大帶動營業利益
10.73 億元（+13.8% QoQ） ；預估稅後淨利季增11.1%至 9.83 億元，EPS 5.87 元，營運創近四年單季新高。

圖一 : Airoha Revenue and Gross Margin Trend Forecast, 1Q25~4Q27 
資料來源:達發，國泰證期研究部整理

光通逐季放量，預期達發 4Q26 營運為近五年同期新高 
達發 4Q26 藍牙音訊進入季節性淡季，惟產品漲價預期於 1Q27 逐步反映，部分客戶提前於 4Q26 備貨，藍牙出貨
衰退幅度可望優於往年 。 光通方面 ， 單通道50G 延續替換需求 ，100G 預期於 4Q26 完成客戶驗證後逐步進入量產 ，
光通營收持續逐季成長；乙太網新一代 10G PHY 於 2H26 量產，並隨 BMC 出貨切入 AI 資料中心 OOB 應用，預
估 4Q26 營收 64.40 億元（-7.2% QoQ），較 4Q25 衰減幅度大幅縮小，淡季不淡。 
毛利率方面，封測價格持續調漲，產品漲價要到 1Q27 才逐步反映，惟高毛利光通占比持續提升，抵銷成本上漲壓
力，預估毛利率持平於49.1%。營業費用 22.05 億元 （-5.6% QoQ） ，營業利益9.57 億元 （-10.8% QoQ，+100.6% 
YoY） ，預估稅後淨利8.99 億元（-8.6% QoQ，+68.8% YoY），EPS 5.36 元，營運為近五年同期新高。

### Page 3

光通營收大幅成長，預期達發 26 年營運創近五年新高 
達發 26 年光通營收受惠可插拔式光收發模組需求增加、客戶規模擴大及替換需求，預期成長至25 年的四倍以上，
占整體營收比重達 mid-single digit， 其中單通道50G 為主要貢獻 ， 搭配出貨的TIA 搭載率亦持續提升 。 寬頻方面 ，
歐美運營商規格升級帶動 10G PON 預估占寬頻營收比重接近三成，10G PON ASP 約為 G-PON 的兩倍，推升產
品組合優化，並切入 AI 資料中心 OOB 架構；乙太網受惠低軌衛星客戶由 1G 升級至 ASP 較高的 2.5G PHY，加上
交換器市占提升，為成長僅次於光通的產品線；藍牙音訊受惠商務耳機專案落地，高階應用占比提升，預估 26 年
營收年增 20.1%至 251.26 億元，創歷史新高。 
毛利率方面，記憶體、NOR Flash 及封測成本上漲壓抑下半年毛利率，惟光通、10G PON 及商務耳機等高價值產
品占比提升，部分抵銷成本壓力，預估 26 年毛利率年減 1.5 個百分點至 50.4%。營業費用 88.75 億元（+11.5% 
YoY） ，營收規模擴大帶動費用率由25 年的 38.0%降至 35.3%，營業利益 37.90 億元（+30.3% YoY） ；預估稅後淨
利年增 24.4%至 35.16 億元，EPS 20.95 元，營運創近五年新高。

圖二 : Airoha Revenue and Gross Margin Trend Forecast, 2022~2027

資料來源:達發，國泰證期研究部整理

預期 100G 擴大放量，達發 27 年營運為歷史新高 
達發 27 年光通動能由單通道 100G 接棒，100G 已於多家模組廠開案，800G 方案達到指標客戶低功耗要求，預期
27 年逐季放量，100G DSP ASP預估約為 50G 的兩倍；單通道50G 受惠替換需求維持穩健成長，100G 搭配的 TIA
搭載率預期高於 50G，並開始單獨出貨搭配其他供應商的 DSP。預期 27 年光通營收較 26 年再成長三倍以上，占
營收比重提升至一成五左右 。 寬頻方面 ，25G PON 預期於 27 年量產 ，OOB 架構市場規模於 26 至 28 年逐年翻倍 ，
預估資料中心 PON 設備市場 26 至 30 年 CAGR 達 52%；乙太網新一代 10G PHY 貢獻提升，2.5G 交換器亦將放
量，預估 27 年營收年增 17.9%至 296.21 億元，創歷史新高。 
毛利率方面，產品價格結構調整逐步反映成本變動，加上高毛利光通占比提升，預估 27 年毛利率年增 0.6 個百分
點至 51.0%。研發費用隨 100G、200G SerDes 及 25G PON 等新產品持續投入，惟 200G SerDes IP 授權金認列
為無形資產並分期攤銷，對費用影響有限，預估營業費用 98.51 億元（+11.0% YoY） ，費用率降至33.3%，營業利
益 52.49 億元（+38.5% YoY） ；預估稅後淨利年增35.3%至 47.60 億元，EPS 28.36 元，營運創歷史新高。

### Page 4

光通與 OOB 切入 AI 資料中心，達發成長動能升溫 
達發 3Q26 受惠光通放量與商務耳機出貨暢旺，預期營運創近四年單季新高；4Q26 光通持續成長，營運為近五年
同期新高。26 年光通營收預期成長至 25 年的四倍以上，10G PON 占寬頻營收比重接近三成，並與乙太網 PHY 同
步切入 AI 資料中心 OOB 架構；27 年單通道 100G 接棒放量，25G PON 與新一代 10G PHY 開始貢獻，產品應用
由消費性電子與家用寬頻逐步延伸至 AI 資料中心光通訊及帶外管理，預估 26 年及 27 年 EPS 分別為 20.95 元及
28.36 元，成長動能明確，初次評等給予買進投資建議。 
目前達發股價為 783 元(28X FY27E EPS)，位於近四年本益比參考區間 20-50 倍中間偏下位置。考量光通進入高速
成長期，預期 27 年營收占比提升至一成五，100G DSP ASP 約為 50G 的兩倍，高毛利產品占比提高帶動 27 年營
業利益年增 38.5%；加上 OOB 架構市場規模預期於 26 至 28 年逐年翻倍，資料中心 PON 設備市場 26 至 30 年
CAGR 預估達 52%，寬頻與乙太網同步受惠 AI 資料中心建置，產品組合轉型方向明確，以近四年本益比參考區間
平均值 35 倍作為評價基礎，目標價 995 元(35x FY27E EPS)。

產業概況 
AI 資料中心頻寬倍增，PAM4 DSP 隨單通道速率升級 
可插拔式光收發模組為 AI 資料中心 Scale-out 網路中伺服器與交換器間資料傳輸的主要介面 ， 模組內由PAM4 DSP
負責訊號等化與時脈回復，TIA 將光檢測器輸出的電流訊號轉換為電壓訊號，Driver 則負責驅動雷射。PAM4 調變
每個符號承載 2 位元，使單通道傳輸速率由 50G 逐步升級至 100G 及 200G，對應光模組容量由 200G、400G 提
升至 800G 與 1.6T。隨 GPU 叢集規模擴大與交換器頻寬倍增，2025 年 800G PAM4晶片組銷售額成長逾一倍，預
估 1.6T 晶片組 2026 年銷售額將超過 20 億美元。 
不同於過往世代全面切換，800G 與 1.6T 預期將並行部署，加上既有 AI 資料中心光模組逐步進入替換期，單通道
50G 與 100G 需求仍具支撐。晶片價值隨速率提升，單通道 100G DSP ASP 預估約為 50G 的兩倍；惟 2027 年後
線性驅動(LPO)與共封裝光學(CPO)滲透率提升，PAM4 DSP成長斜率預期趨緩，而近封裝光學(NPO)若光引擎不含
DSP，訊號補償將更仰賴 TIA 與 Driver，提升高速類比晶片的重要性。

圖三 : 光收發模組與主動式線材 IC 晶片組銷售預估，2025~2031F 
資料來源:LightCounting，國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 5

PON 世代升級加速，OOB 開啟資料中心新應用 
被動光纖網路(PON)以光纖一對多傳輸，由局端 OLT 經無源分光器連接多個終端 ONU，為光纖到府的主流技術。
規格由 G-PON(下行 2.5Gbps)升級至 10G PON，並朝 25G PON 發展，網通世代轉換週期約五至七年；10G PON 
ASP 預估約為 G-PON 的兩倍，隨歐美運營商規格升級與東南亞滲透率提升，帶動 PON 晶片價值增加。 
AI 資料中心除負責運算的主網路外，另需獨立的帶外管理(OOB)網路監控伺服器狀態，以確保運算不中斷。傳統架
構需於每個機櫃頂端安裝管理型以太網交換器，並以銅線一對一連接伺服器，占用 1 至數個機櫃空間單位(RU)；導
入 PON 後，以免供電的無源分光器取代交換器，依實測數據可節省高達 99%的管理機櫃空間，並精簡布線與降低
功耗，讓機櫃容納更多運算節點。超大型雲端業者已陸續導入，預估資料中心PON 設備市場 26 至 30 年 CAGR 達
52%，累計支出逾 30 億美元；伺服器內 BMC 亦需以太網 PHY 傳輸管理訊號，帶動 PON 與 PHY 同步受惠。

圖四 : 超大型雲端業者資料中心 PON-based OOB 架構 
資料來源:Meta，國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 6

公司簡介 
達發科技股份有限公司(Airoha Technology Corp., 6526 TT)成立於 2001 年，總部位於新竹縣竹北市，為聯發科集
團旗下 IC 設計公司。2021 年整併誠致科技、雷凌科技、絡達科技及聯發科IoT 部門並更名為達發科技，2022 年 6
月登錄興櫃，2023 年 10 月於證交所上市。公司業務分為有線網通基礎建設與無線邊緣 AI 兩大事業群：有線網通
涵蓋固網寬頻(G-PON、10G PON)、乙太網(PHY 與交換器晶片)及光通訊(PAM4 DSP 與 TIA)，應用由家用寬頻、
低軌衛星地面設備延伸至 AI 資料中心光通訊與帶外管理，並於 2024 年 7 月完成參與九暘私募，九暘為合併子公
司，布局 PoE 晶片；無線邊緣 AI 以藍牙音訊為主，聚焦商務、電競及聲學等中高階耳機市場，並延伸至藍牙傳輸
(電競鍵鼠、電子貨架標籤)及衛星定位(GNSS)晶片。預估 26 年有線網通與無線邊緣 AI 營收占比約各半，其中藍牙
約 40%、固網寬頻約 30%、乙太網約 14%、衛星定位約 10%、光通約 6%。

圖六 : 2026E 產品營收占比預估

### Page 7

ESG 政策 
達發以社會、經濟、環境及資訊揭露四大構面推動永續經營，已在員工照護、職業安全、董事會獨立性、資
訊安全及溫室氣體管理等面向建立相關制度，評估期間未發生重大人權、消費者權益、職業災害、誠信經營
及環境法規違規情事。

社會面：達發依循國際人權宣言制定人權政策並揭露執行結果，並將人權要求納入供應商評鑑與稽核。員工
管理方面，提供優於法規的休假、保險及身心健康措施，並以員工認股權與持股信託強化人才留任；職業安
全衛生管理系統已取得外部認證，並揭露工傷統計及減災目標。另建立績效評估及能力發展計畫，設定員工
多元化目標、揭露女性主管占比，並足額進用身心障礙員工。未來可完善人權盡職調查流程，並揭露員工滿
意度、訓練成效、性別薪酬比及客戶滿意度。

經濟面：達發每年召開 2 次以上法人說明會，董事長親自出席股東常會，並揭露議案表決結果。獨立董事席
次達半數以上，設置永續發展相關委員會並由董事長擔任最高負責人，且定期執行董事會外部績效評估。另
制定反貪腐、反競爭、內線交易防範及檢舉制度，風險管理政策經董事會通過，並設置資安專責單位、定期
測試資安機制。後續可強化接班規劃、設置提名委員會、提高任一性別董事席次至三分之一以上，並將永續
績效納入高階主管與董事薪酬。

環境面：氣候管理由董事會及管理階層監督，已揭露範疇一、二及範疇三排放，並設定量化中長期減碳、節
能及再生能源目標。達發亦制定水資源及廢棄物管理政策，揭露用水量及有害廢棄物產出量，並將環境表現
納入供應商遴選及稽核。惟尚未取得 ISO 14001 及碳盤查認證，未來可補充 1.5°C 以下及 3°C 以上氣候情境
分析、再生能源使用量及用水、廢棄物量化減量目標。

揭露面：達發依循 GRI 準則編製永續報告書，揭露重大性分析及 SDGs 對應作為，並提供英文版報告書及英
文網站。後續可導入 SASB 對應揭露及第三方確信，建立利害關係人回饋機制，並採用雙重重大性原則強化
ESG 資訊完整性。

《東協尚未擺脫整理，美印關係改善訊號浮現》
9月MSCI東協指數下跌 2.6%，雖然全球公債殖利率
與油價走升帶來評價面壓力 ，但從股價跌幅及外資
賣超規模來看 ，本波修正仍偏向短線漲多後的技術
性調節。修正後，東協股市本益比已由高於近兩年
平均2倍標準差回落至約 1倍標準差 ，RSI亦降至40
以下，顯示市場過熱情況明顯改善 。然而在聯準會
升息及殖利率維持高檔的背景下 ，若基本面未能提
供更多上修空間 ，預期股市短期仍將維持震盪整理
格局。印度方面，9月回檔已使股價回測 3月低點，
本益比降至 19.9倍，RSI跌破30，顯示短線賣壓已

有過度反應。此外，莫迪於9月金磚峰會公開反對去
美元化，近期川普亦表態支持印歐經濟走廊 ，反映
美印經貿關係出現改善訊號 。後續可持續關注雙邊
合作進展及其對印度中長期投資影響。
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望
東南亞 中性 估值及RSI已回到相對合理位置，但殖利率比原先預期更高，基本面需要看到優於預期的表現才有上調空間。
印度 正向 估值及RSI呈現超賣，近期美印雙方經貿合作釋出改善訊號，接下來可開始關注實質政策進展，及相關投資機會(投資與基礎建設概念)

《外資9月轉為小幅賣超，呼應8月轉中立看法》
8月下調東協評等至中立 ，主要考量 6至8月股市累
積漲幅已大致反映第三季經濟維持穩健成長的預期 ，
在缺乏新的催化劑下 ，市場進一步上行空間受限 。
進入9月後，美國公債殖利率與國際油價同步走升，
使市場重新評估高利率與成本壓力對企業獲利的影
響，並加大非科技類股及非科技市場的修正幅度 。
不過，相較於美國公債殖利率持續創高所帶來的壓
力，東協股市 9月僅下跌 2.6%，跌幅仍屬溫和；從
資金面來看 ，外資雖轉為淨賣超 ，但賣超規模明顯
低於第二季調整期間 ，顯示本波修正較偏向漲多後
的獲利了結與資產重新配置 ，而非反映市場對區域
基本面的明顯轉弱或出現恐慌性撤資。

《出口支撐成長敘事未變 ，但高通膨也比預期持久 》
東協及印度經濟仍展現韌性，多數國家8月出口維持
雙位數成長且高於長期平均 ，與全球製造業擴張趨
勢相符 ，其中新加坡及馬來西亞受惠 AI強勁資本支
出，8月出口成長年增率逾 5成，顯示外需仍是重要
成長支撐 。強勁的出口與製造活動不僅有助支撐企
業獲利 ，也在一定程度上抵銷高利率對消費與投資
的壓力 。另一方面 ，印尼、菲律賓等先前高通膨國
家雖已進入降溫趨勢 ，但通膨回落速度仍較緩慢 ，
使央行在降息時程上維持審慎 。整體而言 ，目前東
協與印度景氣尚未出現明顯轉弱 ，但高利率環境可
能維持較市場預期更久 ，意味短期經濟成長動能將
更仰賴外需及製造業活動的支撐。

《川普表態支持印歐經濟走廊 ，重構印度樞紐角色 》
川普近期表態支持印歐經濟走廊 (IMEC)，使這項拜
登任內支持，但因加薩戰爭與區域衝突而停滯近3年
的計畫再度受到市場關注 。隨著中東地緣風險升溫 ，
紅海及荷姆茲海峽等關鍵航道不確定性提高 ，各國
對替代性能源與運輸路徑的需求同步增加 。從規劃
路線來看 ，印歐經濟走廊以印度為起點 ，串連海灣
產油國與歐洲市場 ，若計畫順利推進 ，除有助印度
取得更穩定的中東能源供給 、降低對俄羅斯石油的
依賴外 ，也將進一步鞏固其作為全球製造與供應鏈
樞紐的地位 。對印股而言 ，IMEC有望同時強化能源
安全與供應鏈地位 ，鞏固印度在全球製造的地位 ，
並為基建、工業及製造類股帶來中長期題材支撐。

重點摘要
• 川普表態支持擱置近 3年的印
歐經濟走廊重啟 ，在中東地
緣風險升溫下 ，有助印度強
化能源安全 、降低對俄油依
賴，並鞏固全球製造與供應
鏈樞紐地位。

擴張。所幸，歐洲企業獲利成長穩健將提供歐股下
檔支撐，維持歐股中立評等。

《歐元區經濟較預期具韌性，通膨壓力上升》
歐元區經濟在面臨中東衝突引發的能源成本飆升
下仍具韌性 。最新公布的9月歐元區綜合 PMI初值

升至53.1，超出預期 ，其中 ，製造業 PMI持平在
52.7，服務業PMI回升至53.0。個別國家中，德國
綜合PMI初值回升至 53.8，以2025年10月以來最
快速度擴張 ，AI與國防支出增加提供推動力 。然
而，能源價格居高不下 、天然氣價格漲幅更甚原
油，加大歐元區通膨壓力 ，投入成本和產出價格
均以四個月來最大幅度上漲。
從美伊雙方的態度來看 ，中東地緣僵局以及由此
帶來的能源價格壓力可能會持續一段時間 。歐元
區7月工業生產與零售銷售數據均較為疲軟 ，若能
源價格持續維持高檔 ，恐將推升企業成本並壓縮
家庭實質所得，對製造業與消費需求帶來壓力。
整體而言 ，歐元區經濟仍具韌性 ，但能源價格上
升所帶來的通膨與需求壓力 ，將是後續景氣的重
要觀察變數。
